# Rule Engine → Product Surface Current Wave A Checkpoint

Source state: `wavea-current-working-tree-checkpoint` (2026-09-27). This is a source checkpoint, not a deployed-release claim. Audit taxonomy is frozen by `scripts/audits/build_rule_surfacing_inventory.py`.

## Current Wave A scope

The focused materializer reaches **18** detector groups. Its projection tracks **32** stable rule IDs, of which **29** can currently be emitted by detectors. The remaining historical IDs have explicit unavailable evaluations: `radiation_oncology.same_day_collapse.hyperthermia`, `tablo15_22_exam_admission_date_mismatch`, `tablo15_9_admission_billing_window`.

Only **7** focused IDs are candidates for confirmed, unpriced claim compliance after source and input gates. They enter the in-memory generic projection, but the current patient, bill, and cohort APIs hide them until the complete-25-v1 activation gate. Focused review and financial-review evidence is a draft persisted capability and remains hidden by the same gate. It contributes no confirmed TRY amount or existing report total. This checkpoint does not certify a complete 25-family release.

Relative to the 2026-09-18 baseline, the runtime row count changes from **73** to **80**: seven explicit ownership, materializer, batch, and projection stages were added, with no focused detector removed. The distinct ID/kind count changes from **1,924** to **1,933** because twelve specific radiation identities replace one generic radiation kind, while the corrected admission-window and exam-date detectors no longer claim to emit findings. Those two historical identities remain in the 32-ID unavailable evaluation roster.

## Exact counts

1. Registry rule families: **46**
2. Runtime detectors/adapters: **80**
3. Distinct emitted rule IDs/kinds: **1933**
4. Patient-visible findings: **1393**
5. Cohort-visible findings: **1298**
6. Hidden runtime findings: **535**
7. Aggregate-only findings: **5**
8. Duplicate-surfaced findings: **1392**
9. Financial findings: **359**
10. Nonfinancial findings: **1574**

## Canonical claim-compliance architecture

Canonical evaluator stages: `analyze_claim:evaluate_rule_findings`, `compute_patient_billing_report:evaluate_rule_findings`.
Generic patient projection: `compute_patient_billing_report:project_claim_compliance`.
Generic cohort projection: `src/bilun/domain/billing/cohort_compliance.py::_claim_projection`.
serialized RuleFinding/RuleEvaluation rows are projected generically; no rule-ID allowlist controls patient or cohort projection.

Registry coverage breakdown: `{'implemented': 36, 'non_algorithmic': 6, 'partial': 4}`. Registry rows are metadata and do not satisfy finding visibility.

## Audit interpretation

`analyze_claim` and `compute_patient_billing_report` are independent canonical runtime roots. A hidden `analyze_claim` output is not counted as a hidden semantic finding when the same emitted ID is verified visible through a billing adapter; the semantic rollup de-duplicates those IDs. Independent rerun overlap is recorded separately from duplicate product surfacing. Aggregate-only report blocks remain separate from hidden row-level findings. Rule Database/registry visibility is catalog metadata only.

Patient/cohort visibility classifications remain explicit audit metadata where architectural judgment is required. The generator validates that every patient-report runtime callable has exactly one current metadata entry, that referenced UI/API tokens still exist in source, and that recommended-action duplicate groups still map to emitted backend action surfaces and frontend deep links. It does not treat a shared evidence-file list as proof.

The artifact-only inventory is intentionally bounded to top-level `data/gazette/prose_rules/*.json` files that contain both a `rule_set_id` and a `rules` list. Golden fixtures, schemas, support lookups, and manifests are evidence artifacts rather than independent runtime finding families.
